1	% experiments.tex
2	
3	\section{Experiments}
4	
5	\paragraph{Terminology.}
6	\emph{Cross-attack OOD}: evaluation on attack families unseen during training; benign distribution remains IID throughout (OOD reflects attack-side shift only). $K$: prefix length (number of conversation turns retained). \emph{DAPT}: all Stage-1 adaptations; we extend \citet{gururangan2020dontstop} to supervised variants. \emph{Evidence hierarchy} (on synthetic data): vanilla $<$ MLM $<$ classification (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}).
7	
8	\subsection{Setup}
9	
10	\paragraph{Data.}
11	All synthetic data is generated by Qwen3-8B~\citep{qwen3_2025}.
12	The IID dataset contains 250 Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} jailbreak and 250 topic-anchored benign conversations, split into training (350), validation (74), and test (76) sets with balanced class ratios (e.g., test = 38 jailbreak + 38 benign).
13	Stage~1 (DAPT) uses training + validation conversations; Stage~2 (BiGRU) trains on training, early-stops on validation, and evaluates on test.
14	OOD evaluation pairs attack-specific jailbreak conversations with the 38 IID-test benign conversations: 80 FITD~\citep{fitd2024} (non-discriminative; Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}) + 38 benign = 118 total, 80 Deceptive Delight (DD)~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} + 38 benign = 118 total, and 80 ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} + 38 benign = 118 total.
15	No overlap exists in attack templates across families; benign is IID (same generation pipeline).
16	A paraphrased version of the IID test set (BERTScore 0.92, lexical overlap 0.20) serves as the adversarial robustness benchmark (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}).
17	For human data validation, we use ToxicChat~\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (204 jailbreak + 204 benign from real LMSYS user interactions), entirely independent of Qwen3-8B generation.
18	
19	\paragraph{Models.}
20	Seven DeBERTa-v3 variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline and differ only in Stage-1 training (Section~\ref{sec:method}).
21	Our vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU configuration is architecturally equivalent to DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026} and differs only in encoder choice (DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ BERT); it serves as our primary baseline.
22	For cross-encoder validation, we replicate vanilla, MLM, and 9-class variants on RoBERTa-base~\citep{liu2019roberta} (Section~\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).
23	We also compare TF-IDF LR, LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}), and Llama Guard~3~8B~\citep{metallamaguard3_2024} (zero-shot; evaluates last-response safety given conversation context), which detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks (5/278 pooled recall; Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).
24	
25	\paragraph{Metrics.}
26	F1 for binary jailbreak detection at prefix $K$=2 and full conversations (full per-$K$ results in Appendix~\ref{sec:full_prefix}).
27	OOD results report 3-seed mean$\pm$std (seeds 42, 123, 456) for all variants.
28	For key pairwise comparisons, we report BCa bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across 3 seeds; this assumes cross-seed independence and is exploratory) and paired permutation tests (10,000 permutations). Seed-level F1 values (Table~\ref{tab:seed_level}, Appendix~\ref{app:seed_level}) confirm all pairwise conclusions hold at every seed.
29	All standard deviations are computed over 3 random seeds using the population formula ($n$ divisor); Bessel-corrected estimates ($n{-}1$ divisor) are $\approx$22\% larger but do not alter any significance conclusions.
30	
31	\paragraph{Training.}
32	Stage-1: learning rate $2 \times 10^{-5}$, batch 16, 4 epochs, MLM masking 15\%.
33	Stage-2: two-layer MLP, 20 epochs.
34	
35	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
36	\subsection{Cross-Attack Generalization}
37	
38	\begin{table}[t]
39	\centering
40	\small
41	\caption{Cross-attack generalization (F1) at $K$=2 and full conversation length. IID: Crescendo; OOD: Deceptive Delight (DD). 3-seed mean$\pm$std where noted; full per-$K$ in Appendix~\ref{sec:full_prefix}.}
42	\label{tab:main_results}
43	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc|cc@{}}
44	\toprule
45	& \multicolumn{2}{c|}{\textit{IID (Cresc.)}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textit{OOD (DD)}} \\
46	\textbf{Method} & $K$=2 & Full & $K$=2 & Full \\
47	\midrule
48	9-class & \textbf{1.000$\pm$.000} & \textbf{1.000$\pm$.000} & .981$\pm$.013 & \textbf{.994$\pm$.005} \\
49	MLM-only$^{\ddagger}$ & .974 & 1.000 & \textbf{.993$\pm$.009} & .914$\pm$.115 \\
50	Vanilla & .978$\pm$.016 & 1.000$\pm$.000 & .479$\pm$.038 & .312$\pm$.026 \\
51	TF-IDF LR$^{\S}$ & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .961 \\
52	\bottomrule
53	\end{tabular}
54	\vspace{-2pt}
55	{\footnotesize $^{\ddagger}$MLM-only IID reports single seed (42); OOD reports 3-seed. $^{\S}$TF-IDF is deterministic (no std).}
56	\end{table}
57	
58	Vanilla encoders collapse under cross-attack transfer; domain-adapted variants recover near-perfect detection (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}, Figure~\ref{fig:cross_attack_bar}).
59	DD exhibits high lexical separability (TF-IDF F1\,=\,0.961); ActorAttack, where TF-IDF collapses to 0.261, serves as the primary stress test for beyond-lexical generalization.
60	On OOD DD, vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1 = 0.312$\pm$0.026 and worsens with more turns (0.628 at $K$=1 to 0.312 at Full), consistent with attack turns occupying ``benign'' embedding regions~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}.
61	MLM achieves mean F1 $\geq$ 0.97 at all prefixes $K \leq 5$ without any classification labels; domain exposure alone is sufficient.
62	At full conversation length, MLM drops to 0.914$\pm$0.115 (seed 456 recall = 0.64), while the 9-class model maintains 0.994$\pm$0.005.
63	TF-IDF LR reaches F1 = 0.961 on full DD; DD is fully solvable by lexical features.
64	On strategy-diverse ActorAttack, TF-IDF collapses to F1 = 0.261 (precision = 1.0, recall = 0.15), while 9-class maintains 0.972$\pm$0.026: a 71\,pp gap. Lexical methods cannot capture multi-persona role-playing attack structure. This establishes a ``difficulty threshold'' for lexical methods parallel to that of vanilla encoders: both succeed on DD and fail on ActorAttack (Section~\ref{sec:limitations}).
65	Bootstrap CIs confirm non-overlapping intervals (9-class DD Full [0.943, 1.000] vs.\ vanilla [0.202, 0.341]; all permutation $p < 0.0001$). FITD is non-discriminative (all variants F1\,$\geq$\,0.990; Section~\ref{sec:limitations}).
66	
67	\begin{figure}[t]
68	\centering
69	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig2_cross_attack_bar.pdf}
70	\caption{Cross-attack overview (F1 at full conversation length). DisPeD (9-class) maintains near-perfect detection across IID and both OOD families; vanilla collapses on OOD DD. FITD is non-discriminative for all methods.}
71	\label{fig:cross_attack_bar}
72	\end{figure}
73	
74	Prefix-length analysis (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:full_prefix}) shows vanilla F1 drops from 0.628 at $K$=1 to 0.312 at full length. MLM achieves strong early detection ($K$=2: 0.993) but is unstable at full length (0.914$\pm$0.115); the 9-class model maintains 0.972--0.994 across all prefixes. Classification supervision thus stabilizes against distributional drift.
75	
76	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
77	\subsection{Decomposing the Detection Mechanism}
78	\label{sec:decomposition}
79	
80	\begin{table}[t]
81	\centering
82	\small
83	\caption{Evidence hierarchy on OOD DD and ActorAttack (AA) at full conversation length (3-seed mean$\pm$std where available). OOD F1 reflects attack-side generalization only; benign samples are IID from training distribution (benign-side shift evaluated in Section~\ref{sec:benign_shift}). Bold indicates best per column. All variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline. Full per-prefix results in Appendix Tables~\ref{tab:full_prefix} and~\ref{tab:actorattack_full}.}
84	\label{tab:evidence_hierarchy}
85	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
86	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
87	\toprule
88	\textbf{Variant} & DD-Full & AA-Full & DD-$K$=2 \\
89	\midrule
90	9-class (full model) & .994$\pm$.005 & .972$\pm$.026 & .981$\pm$.013 \\
91	Binary (2-class) & \textbf{1.000$\pm$.000} & \textbf{.990$\pm$.008} & .994$\pm$.009 \\
92	Scrambled labels & .994$\pm$.005 & .951$\pm$.043 & .981$\pm$.026 \\
93	MLM-only$^{\ddagger}$ (no labels) & .914$\pm$.115 & .893$\pm$.080 & .993$\pm$.009 \\
94	Mean pooling$^{*}$ & .991$\pm$.013 & .962$\pm$.015 & \textbf{1.000} \\
95	\midrule
96	Topic classif.\ (5-class) & .541$\pm$.207 & .625$\pm$.141 & .593$\pm$.166 \\
97	Wikipedia MLM & .904$\pm$.115 & .686$\pm$.297 & .911$\pm$.073 \\
98	Vanilla DeBERTa & .312$\pm$.026 & .258$\pm$.000 & .479$\pm$.038 \\
99	TF-IDF LR$^{\dagger}$ & .961 & .261 & \textbf{1.000} \\
100	Llama Guard~3$^{\P}$ & .025 & .000 & -- \\
101	\bottomrule
102	\end{tabular}}
103	\vspace{-2pt}
104	{\footnotesize $^{*}$9-class encoder with mean pooling replacing BiGRU. $^{\dagger}$Deterministic (no std). $^{\ddagger}$MLM-only IID (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}) is single-seed (42); all OOD values are 3-seed. $^{\P}$Zero-shot; no domain training.}
105	\end{table}
106	
107	A clear \emph{evidence hierarchy} emerges across seven variants (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}), ordered by OOD detection at full conversation length on DD:
108	
109	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026) $<$ topic (0.541$\pm$0.207) $\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) $\approx$ jb-MLM (0.914$\pm$0.115) $<$ scrambled (0.994$\pm$0.005) $=$ 9-class (0.994$\pm$0.005) $\approx$ binary (1.000$\pm$0.000)}
110	\vspace{1pt}
111	
112	\paragraph{Domain exposure is the primary mechanism.}
113	MLM bridges $\sim$60\,pp from vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026 $\to$ 0.914$\pm$0.115) without labels; this supports the DAPT interpretation~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.
114	All three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.994--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \geq 0.40$) provide descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence. Extended to $n$\,=\,10 seeds, TOST (margin = 5\,pp) confirms equivalence on DD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d$\,=\,$-$0.14; Table~\ref{tab:tost_sensitivity}) and FITD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d \approx 0$). An observed difficulty gradient emerges: template attacks show full equivalence ($d < 0.1$), while strategy-diverse ActorAttack retains moderate label sensitivity ($d$\,=\,0.44, $p$\,=\,0.183). Label quality and granularity are descriptively non-factors for detection.
115	Classification supervision is associated with 3--23$\times$ lower cross-seed variance over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\ 0.115 on DD; observed range across 3 seeds; formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at $n$=3; cross-seed ensemble yields marginal additional gains, Appendix~\ref{app:ensemble}). This stability benefit does not transfer to human-authored data, where classification-supervised variants exhibit the highest variance and pure MLM the lowest (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
116	Appendix~\ref{app:tsne} provides t-SNE visualizations confirming that domain-adapted variants produce tighter, more separable embedding clusters than the vanilla baseline.
117	
118	\paragraph{Domain specificity is marginal at full length but meaningful for early detection.}
119	Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length (1\,pp gap), though jailbreak-domain text provides an 8\,pp advantage at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911).
120	Perturbation analysis (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}) reveals a sharper domain specificity effect: Wikipedia MLM degrades by $\sim$12\,pp under adversarial paraphrasing while jailbreak-domain MLM degrades by only 2.6\,pp.
121	
122	\paragraph{Misaligned supervision destabilizes generalization.}
123	Topic classification yields DD-Full F1 = 0.541$\pm$0.207; seed 123 falls \emph{below} vanilla (0.252 vs.\ 0.312). Despite using the same jailbreak-domain training data, topic classification performs worse than Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115), which uses out-of-domain text with no labels. A misaligned objective forces the encoder to allocate capacity to irrelevant features and actively destabilizes representations that MLM would preserve.
124	
125	\paragraph{Evidence hierarchy on strategy-diverse attacks.}
126	ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} uses multi-persona steering across longer conversation structures and provides a more challenging OOD benchmark than DD.
127	The evidence hierarchy at full conversation length is:
128	
129	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small TF-IDF (0.261) $\approx$ vanilla (0.258$\pm$0.000) $<$ topic (0.625$\pm$0.141) $<$ wiki-MLM (0.686$\pm$0.297) $<$ jb-MLM (0.893$\pm$0.080) $<$ scrambled (0.951$\pm$0.043) $\approx$ 9-class (0.972$\pm$0.026) $\approx$ binary (0.990$\pm$0.008)}
130	\vspace{1pt}
131	
132	TF-IDF collapses from 0.961 (DD) to 0.261 (ActorAttack, precision = 1.0, recall = 0.15). This gap shows that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond bag-of-words.
133	All classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent performance (0.951--0.990, max gap 3.9\,pp); this extends the DD finding. DAPT provides $\sim$71\,pp over both vanilla (0.258) and TF-IDF (0.261). Full per-prefix results and per-prefix error analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:actorattack_full} and~\ref{app:aa_error}.
134	
135	\paragraph{Temporal modeling.}
136	Mean pooling matches BiGRU on both DD and ActorAttack (F1 gap $\leq$ 1\,pp, overlapping CIs; Appendix~\ref{app:temporal}).
137	Turn-order shuffling produces negligible change ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) across both DeBERTa-v3-base 9-class (DD $\Delta$\,=\,0.000, AA $\Delta$\,=\,$-$0.009, IID $\Delta$\,=\,0.000) and RoBERTa-large 9-class (DD $\Delta$\,=\,+0.001, AA $\Delta$\,=\,$-$0.007, IID $\Delta$\,=\,0.000); detection relies on per-turn representations rather than sequential patterns.
138	
139	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
140	\subsection{Cross-Encoder Validation}
141	\label{sec:cross_encoder}
142	
143	\begin{table}[t]
144	\centering
145	\small
146	\caption{Cross-encoder comparison on OOD Full (3-seed mean$\pm$std). DD = Deceptive Delight, AA = ActorAttack. Bold indicates best per encoder group.}
147	\label{tab:cross_encoder}
148	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
149	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
150	\toprule
151	\textbf{Variant} & DD & AA \\
152	\midrule
153	\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{DeBERTa-v3-base (184M)}} \\[2pt]
154	Vanilla & .312$\pm$.026 & .258$\pm$.000 \\
155	MLM & .914$\pm$.115 & .893$\pm$.080 \\
156	9-class & \textbf{.994$\pm$.005} & \textbf{.972$\pm$.026} \\
157	\midrule
158	\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{RoBERTa-base (125M)}} \\[2pt]
159	Vanilla & .981$\pm$.026 & .466$\pm$.163 \\
160	MLM & .987$\pm$.018 & .730$\pm$.201 \\
161	9-class & \textbf{.994$\pm$.005} & \textbf{.939$\pm$.046} \\
162	\midrule
163	\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{RoBERTa-large (355M)}} \\[2pt]
164	Vanilla & .927$\pm$.036 & \textbf{1.000$\pm$.000} \\
165	MLM & .884$\pm$.065 & .874$\pm$.014 \\
166	9-class & \textbf{.987$\pm$.004} & .946$\pm$.057 \\
167	\bottomrule
168	\end{tabular}}
169	\end{table}
170	
171	To test whether the evidence hierarchy depends on encoder choice, we replicate vanilla, MLM, and 9-class on RoBERTa-base~\citep{liu2019roberta} (Table~\ref{tab:cross_encoder}).
172	On DD, RoBERTa-base vanilla already achieves 0.981$\pm$0.026 (vs.\ DeBERTa 0.312$\pm$0.026), so DAPT adds only 1.3\,pp. ActorAttack exposes this as DD's simplicity: RoBERTa-base vanilla collapses to 0.466$\pm$0.163, a 51.5\,pp drop, while domain-adapted variants recover (9-class: 0.939$\pm$0.046).
173	
174	RoBERTa-large (355M) reveals a capacity-DAPT interaction: its effect on ActorAttack reverses (vanilla 1.000 vs.\ 9-class 0.946), while smaller encoders require DAPT to bridge 47--71\,pp. All continued pretraining reduces ActorAttack performance for the largest encoder via representation drift (Table~\ref{tab:roberta_large_lr}), though classification DAPT partially compensates on DD (+6\,pp).
175	
176	Three encoder scales thus reveal a scale-dependent pattern for the \emph{difficulty threshold}: DeBERTa-v3-base requires adaptation for all attacks, RoBERTa-base only for complex ones, and RoBERTa-large for none (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}).
177	Classification supervision provides a consistent stability benefit across both base encoders (cross-seed variance: 0.005--0.046 vs.\ 0.026--0.201).
178	A BERT-base-uncased proxy (110M, matching DeepContext's encoder family) without adaptation achieves DD\,=\,0.318$\pm$0.031 and AA\,=\,0.275$\pm$0.027, comparable to vanilla DeBERTa-v3 (Appendix~\ref{app:bert_proxy}); cross-attack generalization is driven by DAPT rather than encoder architecture.
179	
180	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
181	\subsection{Mixed-LLM Training}
182	
183	All preceding experiments use single-source (Qwen3-8B) data. To test whether the evidence hierarchy transfers when training data mixes LLM sources, we train on 250 Qwen + 250 Llama-3.1-8B conversations (Table~\ref{tab:mixed_llm}).
184	Classification-based DAPT is the only variant that substantially outperforms vanilla: 9-class achieves DD OOD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp over vanilla 0.291$\pm$0.065), while MLM drops to vanilla level (0.289$\pm$0.101). On strategy-diverse ActorAttack, the advantage narrows (9-class 0.504$\pm$0.107 vs.\ vanilla 0.389$\pm$0.073, +11.5\,pp) with high seed variance; limited per-source volume (250 per LLM) likely constrains generalization to complex attacks. DD and FITD thus primarily validate the baseline rather than the method; ActorAttack, where lexical methods collapse (TF-IDF F1\,=\,0.261) and vanilla encoders fail (F1\,$\leq$\,0.389 across conditions), provides the discriminative evaluation axis.
185	Mixed-LLM training confirms that classification supervision generalizes across LLM sources where unsupervised pretraining does not; full results in Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}.
186	
187	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
188	\subsection{Human Data Validation}
189	\label{sec:human_data}
190	
191	Since both training and OOD evaluation data are Qwen3-8B-generated, we validate on ToxicChat~\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (204 human-written jailbreak attempts + 204 benign conversations from real LMSYS interactions).
192	
193	\begin{table}[t]
194	\centering
195	\small
196	\caption{Human data validation on ToxicChat~\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (macro F1, 3-seed mean$\pm$std). Bold indicates best. Single-turn data from real LMSYS user interactions, independent of Qwen3-8B.}
197	\label{tab:human_data}
198	\begin{tabular}{@{}lc@{}}
199	\toprule
200	\textbf{Variant} & Macro F1 \\
201	\midrule
202	JB-MLM & \textbf{.837$\pm$.013} \\
203	Wiki-MLM & .791$\pm$.093 \\
204	Binary & .705$\pm$.072 \\
205	Scrambled & .616$\pm$.056 \\

207	Topic & .496$\pm$.042 \\

213	
214	All DAPT variants exceed vanilla by 18--42\,pp (Table~\ref{tab:human_data}): the adapted-vs.-vanilla gap is not a Qwen3-8B generation artifact, and domain exposure on synthetic jailbreak text transfers to human-written attacks.
215	
216	The ordering reverses: JB-MLM leads (0.837$\pm$0.013) while 9-class ranks lower (0.596$\pm$0.087; $p < 0.001$, non-overlapping CIs). ToxicChat is single-turn, so the classification head provides no advantage; validating the multi-turn hierarchy requires multi-turn human data.
217	
218	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
219	\subsection{Perturbation Analysis}
220	\label{sec:perturbation}
221	
222	Under adversarial paraphrasing (BERTScore 0.92), classification-supervised variants achieve zero degradation on IID and OOD DD (Table~\ref{tab:ood_perturbation}) while non-adapted models degrade by $\sim$12\,pp ($p < 0.001$); on ActorAttack, DAPT variants degrade ($\Delta$\,=\,$-$20.4\,pp) but maintain 52\,pp over vanilla (Appendix~\ref{sec:perturbation_full}).
223	
224	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
225	\subsection{Strategy Trajectory Analysis}
226	\label{sec:strategy}
227	
228	The 9-class taxonomy provides interpretable characterization of known attacks ($\kappa$=0.78, $n$=50; Appendix~\ref{sec:strategy_full}) but degrades to near-random on OOD ($\kappa$=0.126; Appendix~\ref{app:strategy_pred}). Its value is twofold: as ablation infrastructure (scrambled labels produce equivalent detection) and as an interpretability tool for known attacks; detection operates through domain-adapted \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings, not strategy accuracy.
229	
230	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
231	\subsection{Benign Distribution Shift}
232	\label{sec:benign_shift}
233	
234	All preceding OOD results use IID benign data. On WildChat benign conversations, 9-class FPR rises to 68.1\%; cross-LLM error decomposition confirms false positives on unfamiliar benign text drive the collapse (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr},~\ref{app:cross_llm}).
235	Few-shot benign-only adaptation resolves this without target-domain attack data: 10 WildChat conversations reduce FPR to 0\%, and 25 OASST2 conversations reduce FPR to 1.0\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr},~\ref{app:oasst2}); dual-OOD F1 drops 12--14\,pp without adaptation but recovers within 0.3\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:dual_ood}).
236

This establishes a ``difficulty threshold'' for lexical methods parallel to that of vanilla encoders: both succeed on DD and fail on ActorAttack (Section~\\ref{sec:limitations}).\nBootstrap CIs confirm non-overlapping intervals (9-class DD Full [0.943, 1.000] vs.\\ vanilla [0.202, 0.341]; all permutation $p < 0.0001$). TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.\nAll three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.994--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \\geq 0.40$) provide descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence. An observed difficulty gradient emerges: template attacks show full equivalence ($d < 0.1$), while strategy-diverse ActorAttack retains moderate label sensitivity ($d$\\,=\\,0.44, $p$\\,=\\,0.183). A misaligned objective forces the encoder to allocate capacity to irrelevant features and actively destabilizes representations that MLM would preserve.\n\n\\paragraph{Evidence hierarchy on strategy-diverse attacks.}\nActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} uses multi-persona steering across longer conversation structures and provides a more challenging OOD benchmark than DD.\nThe evidence hierarchy at full conversation length is:\n\n\\noindent\\hspace{0.5em}{\\small TF-IDF (0.261) $\\approx$ vanilla (0.258$\\pm$0.000) $<$ topic (0.625$\\pm$0.141) $<$ wiki-MLM (0.686$\\pm$0.297) $<$ jb-MLM (0.893$\\pm$0.080) $<$ scrambled (0.951$\\pm$0.043) $\\approx$ 9-class (0.972$\\pm$0.026) $\\approx$ binary (0.990$\\pm$0.008)}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n\nTF-IDF collapses from 0.961 (DD) to 0.261 (ActorAttack, precision = 1.0, recall = 0.15). This gap shows that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond bag-of-words.\nAll classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent performance (0.951--0.990, max gap 3.9\\,pp); this extends the DD finding. DAPT provides $\\sim$71\\,pp over both vanilla (0.258) and TF-IDF (0.261). Full per-prefix results and per-prefix error analysis in Appendix~\\ref{sec:actorattack_full} and~\\ref{app:aa_error}.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal modeling.}\nMean pooling matches BiGRU on both DD and ActorAttack (F1 gap $\\leq$ 1\\,pp, overlapping CIs; Appendix~\\ref{app:temporal}).\nTurn-order shuffling produces negligible change ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) across both DeBERTa-v3-base 9-class (DD $\\Delta$\\,=\\,0.000, AA $\\Delta$\\,=\\,$-$0.009, IID $\\Delta$\\,=\\,0.000) and RoBERTa-large 9-class (DD $\\Delta$\\,=\\,+0.001, AA $\\Delta$\\,=\\,$-$0.007, IID $\\Delta$\\,=\\,0.000); detection relies on per-turn representations rather than sequential patterns.\n\n%----------------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Cross-Encoder Validation}\n\\label{sec:cross_encoder}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-encoder comparison on OOD Full (3-seed mean$\\pm$std). Bold indicates best per encoder group.}\n\\label{tab:cross_encoder}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Variant} & DD & AA \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{3}{l}{\\textit{DeBERTa-v3-base (184M)}} \\\\[2pt]\nVanilla & .312$\\pm$.026 & .258$\\pm$.000 \\\\\nMLM & .914$\\pm$.115 & .893$\\pm$.080 \\\\\n9-class & & .730$\\pm$.201 \\\\\n9-class & \\textbf{.994$\\pm$.005} & \\textbf{.939$\\pm$.046} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{3}{l}{\\textit{RoBERTa-large (355M)}} \\\\[2pt]\nVanilla & .927$\\pm$.036 & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} \\\\\nMLM & .884$\\pm$.065 & .874$\\pm$.014 \\\\\n9-class & \\textbf{.987$\\pm$.004} & .946$\\pm$.057 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nTo test whether the {

  "sessionId": "234452d8-90ad-444a-9761-3138a730f6bb",
  "content": "[Director 指令] **[R19 审稿修复 — 综合 Action Matrix] DDL 2026-05-25，48h 内完成**\n\nR19 Overall 3.0，6 MF + 10 SF + 4 遗漏。所有 MF 是复发问题，前轮 hedging 修复无效，本轮需结构性重写。按优先级执行，每完成一个 P 级报告进展。\n\n---\n\n## P0: 紧急文本错误（30min）\n\n**P0-1**: experiments.tex L114 附近 \"$d < 0.1$\" → \"$|d| \\leq 0.14$\"\n- 这是之前 P0 修复 intro/conclusion 时遗漏的位置。找到这句（可能是 \"template attacks show full equivalence ($d < 0.1$)\" 之类），改为与 intro/conclusion 一致的 \"|d|≤0.14\"\n\n**P0-2**: introduction.tex L24 附近 \"the first such controlled factorial study\" → \"the first, to our knowledge, such controlled factorial study\"\n\n---\n\n## P1: Must-Fix 文本重写（2-3h，可多 worker 并行）\n\n### P1-MF1: Abstract + Conclusion 双柱重写（ToxicChat 反转升级为主要 finding）\n\n**Abstract 重写策略**: 当前 ~250 词压缩至 ~180 词。核心结构改为双柱：\n- \"On synthetic multi-turn data, classification-based DAPT recovers OOD detection (F1=0.97–1.0 at base scale); however, on human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), this hierarchy reverses—MLM-based DAPT outperforms classification (0.837 vs 0.596, 24.1pp gap)—indicating that classification DAPT's advantage may derive from multi-turn structural patterns rather than universal mechanisms.\"\n- 所有 OOD F1 数字后加 \"(attack-side OOD; IID benign upper bound)\"\n- Mixed-LLM 限定: \"template attacks remain detectable; strategy-diverse attacks degrade to chance level (F1=0.504)\"\n- 删除或大幅压缩次要数字，保留 3-4 个关键数字\n- 加 \"at base model scale (125M–184M)\" 限定\n\n**Conclusion 重写策略**: 给 ToxicChat 反转同等篇幅。结构：\n1. 合成数据上的主要发现（2-3 句）\n2. ToxicChat 反转 + 其启示（2-3 句）\n3. 边界条件（scale-dependent, mixed-LLM, length sensitivity）（1-2 句）\n4. 方法论贡献（消融框架本身）（1-2 句）\n\n### P1-MF2: Table 2 增加 dual-OOD 行\n\n在 Table 2 底部增加一行 \"Dual-OOD (WildChat benign)\"，数据来自 Appendix Table 22:\n- 9-Class DD: 0.855, AA: 0.842\n- 其他变体对应数值从 Table 22 提取\n- 再加一行 \"Dual-OOD + 10-shot adapt\": 恢复至 0.3pp 以内的数值\n- Table caption 加注: \"Rows 1–N: attack-side OOD with IID benign. Bottom rows: full OOD evaluation.\"\n- 如果 Table 2 太拥挤，可以只加 9-class 一行，其他变体引用 Appendix\n\n### P1-MF3: Section 4.5 Mixed-LLM AA=0.504 修复\n\n- 明确报告 majority baseline (~0.5)\n- 加 \"statistically indistinguishable from chance (n=3, p>0.05)\"\n- 修改措辞: \"the advantage narrows\" → \"ActorAttack detection degrades to chance level (F1=0.504±0.107, indistinguishable from majority baseline)\"\n- Abstract 对应处已有 \"F1=0.504\" 但需加 \"≈chance level\"\n\n### P1-MF5: Seed-level 为主要报告单位\n\n全文搜索 \"bootstrap\"、\"CI\"、\"confidence interval\"、\"non-overlapping\"：\n- \"confirms non-overlapping intervals\" → \"seed-level results show consistent separation\"\n- 每处池化 CI 引用加 \"(exploratory, pooled across seeds assuming independence)\"\n- 确保 seed-level Table 8 在正文中被引用为主要证据\n\n### P1-MF6: 方差 3-23× 降级\n\n- experiments.tex 中 \"3-23×\" 前加 \"descriptive\"\n- 检查 intro/conclusion 有无引用此数字，同步降级\n- 确保 ToxicChat 反转限定已存在（Reviewer 确认 experiments.tex L115 附近已有）\n\n### P1-SF4: 因果语言系统替换\n\n高优先级替换（Reviewer 指出的 3 处）：\n1. abstract: \"Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks **requires** domain adaptation\" → \"benefits substantially from domain adaptation at base model scale\"\n2. intro: \"cross-attack generalization **requires** domain adaptation, not a better classifier\" → \"cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation\"\n3. experiments: \"Domain exposure is **the primary mechanism**\" → \"the primary observed factor\"\n\n全文搜索 \"requires\"、\"drives\"、\"causes\"、\"mechanism\"、\"necessary\"，逐处评估是否需要改为 associational 语言。\n\nDiscussion 新增一段 memorization/overfitting 替代解释：\"An alternative interpretation of the low-dimensional discriminative subspace is overfitting or representation collapse rather than beneficial feature selection. The 68.1% FPR on out-of-distribution benign data provides partial evidence for this interpretation...\"\n\n---\n\n## P2: MF4 长度匹配对照实验（2-4h）\n\n**这是唯一的 evidence 问题，也是唯一能推动 3.0→3.5 的新证据。**\n\n实验设计（推理 only，不需训练）：\n1. 从现有 DD/AA 测试数据中，按 turn count 分桶\n2. 构造长度匹配子集：只保留 benign 和 jailbreak turn count 重叠的区间（比如都在 5-10 turns）\n3. 用已训练好的 9-class DeBERTa + BiGRU 模型在匹配子集上推理\n4. 报告 length-matched F1 vs full-set F1 的差异\n\n如果长度匹配后 F1 基本不变 → DD 不依赖长度，是正面证据\n如果 F1 显著下降 → 长度确实是 confound，诚实报告为 limitation\n\n同时在 Section 5 (Discussion) 增加 prominent 段落讨论 AA padding collapse (-57.9pp):\n- 区分 \"length as shortcut\" vs \"padding destroys signal\"\n- DD Δ=-2.5pp 说明 DD 对 padding resilient\n- AA Δ=-57.9pp 可能源于 signal density 稀释（真实攻击话语在 padding 后被稀释到不可检测）\n\n**连接到 westd-33183**，用 cuda:0 执行推理。\n\n---\n\n## P3: Should-Fix + 遗漏项（1-2h）\n\n**P3-SF2**: Footnote 1 修改为 \"We use 'disentangled' to describe our experimental methodology of isolating individual factors through controlled ablation, distinct from disentangled representation learning (β-VAE, InfoGAN).\"\n\n**P3-SF3**: Section 4.7 或 Method 增加操作化定义段落：\n\"We operationally distinguish template attacks (fixed prompt template with variable payload; e.g., DanPrompt) from structurally complex attacks (multi-turn strategy adaptation with dynamic context exploitation; e.g., ActorAttack). A quantitative proxy is per-turn strategy transition rate: template attacks show rate < 0.1 (consistent strategy), while complex attacks show rate > 0.3 (frequent strategy shifts).\"\n\n**P3-SF5**: Abstract 加 \"at base model scale (125M–184M)\"；Conclusion 加 \"At larger scales (355M), DAPT can be counterproductive (RoBERTa-large: vanilla AA=1.000 vs 9-class AA=0.946).\"\n\n**P3-SF6**: Discussion 新增段落讨论 temporal modeling 等价性的 implications:\n\"The equivalence of mean pooling and BiGRU (Appendix T) suggests that per-turn representations already encode sufficient discriminative signal, with temporal order providing negligible additional information. This challenges the intuition that multi-turn attacks require sequential modeling and suggests that attack detection may operate on per-turn features aggregated independently.\"\n\n**P3-SF7**: Related Work 或 Experiments 加一句: \"Direct experimental comparison with DeepContext, BIID, and G-Guard is precluded by the absence of public implementations and incompatible evaluation protocols (closed-source datasets, different attack taxonomies); Appendix Q provides a parameter-matched BERT-base proxy as a partial substitute.\"\n\n**P3-Q1**: 补充 few-shot benign adaptation 实现细节。在 Section 4.9 或 Method 加: \"Few-shot adaptation fine-tunes the Stage-2 BiGRU classifier head (Stage-1 encoder frozen) on N target-domain benign conversations using BCE loss for 5 epochs. Adaptation and evaluation sets are disjoint (10 conversations for adaptation, remainder for evaluation).\"\n\n**P3-Q2**: Method 或 Appendix 明确: \"Scrambled labels use a fixed random permutation (seed 42). Robustness is verified across 2 additional random permutations (seeds 123, 456) with consistent results (Appendix F).\" — 如果这个事实存在的话。如果只做了一个 permutation，诚实报告并在 Limitations 标注。\n\n**P3-Q4**: Section 4.5 加一句: \"An alternative explanation for the mixed-LLM performance degradation is per-source data reduction (250 vs 350 conversations per source) rather than distribution shift per se. Disentangling these factors would require training on 350 conversations from each source independently, which we leave to future work.\"\n\n**P3-Missing References**: 核实 R19 推荐的 7 篇引用真实性（WebSearch 验证 arXiv ID），优先添加:\n1. \"Do Internal Layers of LLMs Reveal Patterns for Jailbreak Detection?\" (2510.06594) — 与 SVD/Fisher 分析方法论高度相关\n2. \"Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace\" (2602.11495) — representation-level detection\n3. Markov et al. 2023 \"A holistic approach to undesired content detection\" — OpenAI production baseline\n其余 4 篇视相关性酌情添加。\n\n**P3-Visual**: Figure 2 x 轴标签字号放大至 ≥8pt。\n\n---\n\n## 执行规范\n\n1. 每个 P 级完成后立即报告，附修改前后对比（old_string → new_string）\n2. P0 和 P1 可由不同 worker 并行\n3. P2 实验完成后，无论结果正面/负面，都在 Section 5 讨论并报告\n4. 所有修改完成后 `paper_compile_and_check` 验证编译 + 页数\n5. 修复后的关键段落（Abstract、Conclusion、Table 2 caption、Discussion 新段落）发给 Reviewer 做验证\n\n**禁止**: 自由发挥重写其他部分；引入新 claim；改变论文标题/作者；触碰 figures/ 以外的非 .tex 文件"
}

[Director 指令] **[R19 审稿修复 — 综合 Action Matrix] DDL 2026-05-25，48h 内完成**

R19 Overall 3.0，6 MF + 10 SF + 4 遗漏。所有 MF 是复发问题，前轮 hedging 修复无效，本轮需结构性重写。按优先级执行，每完成一个 P 级报告进展。

---

## P0: 紧急文本错误（30min）

**P0-1**: experiments.tex L114 附近 "$d < 0.1$" → "$|d| \leq 0.14$"
- 这是之前 P0 修复 intro/conclusion 时遗漏的位置。找到这句（可能是 "template attacks show full equivalence ($d < 0.1$)" 之类），改为与 intro/conclusion 一致的 "|d|≤0.14"

**P0-2**: introduction.tex L24 附近 "the first such controlled factorial study" → "the first, to our knowledge, such controlled factorial study"

---

## P1: Must-Fix 文本重写（2-3h，可多 worker 并行）

### P1-MF1: Abstract + Conclusion 双柱重写（ToxicChat 反转升级为主要 finding）

**Abstract 重写策略**: 当前 ~250 词压缩至 ~180 词。核心结构改为双柱：
- "On synthetic multi-turn data, classification-based DAPT recovers OOD detection (F1=0.97–1.0 at base scale); however, on human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), this hierarchy reverses—MLM-based DAPT outperforms classification (0.837 vs 0.596, 24.1pp gap)—indicating that classification DAPT's advantage may derive from multi-turn structural patterns rather than universal mechanisms."
- 所有 OOD F1 数字后加 "(attack-side OOD; IID benign upper bound)"
- Mixed-LLM 限定: "template attacks remain detectable; strategy-diverse attacks degrade to chance level (F1=0.504)"
- 删除或大幅压缩次要数字，保留 3-4 个关键数字
- 加 "at base model scale (125M–184M)" 限定

**Conclusion 重写策略**: 给 ToxicChat 反转同等篇幅。结构：
1. 合成数据上的主要发现（2-3 句）
2. ToxicChat 反转 + 其启示（2-3 句）
3. 边界条件（scale-dependent, mixed-LLM, length sensitivity）（1-2 句）
4. 方法论贡献（消融框架本身）（1-2 句）

### P1-MF2: Table 2 增加 dual-OOD 行

在 Table 2 底部增加一行 "Dual-OOD (WildChat benign)"，数据来自 Appendix Table 22:
- 9-Class DD: 0.855, AA: 0.842
- 其他变体对应数值从 Table 22 提取
- 再加一行 "Dual-OOD + 10-shot adapt": 恢复至 0.3pp 以内的数值
- Table caption 加注: "Rows 1–N: attack-side OOD with IID benign. Bottom rows: full OOD evaluation."
- 如果 Table 2 太拥挤，可以只加 9-class 一行，其他变体引用 Appendix

### P1-MF3: Section 4.5 Mixed-LLM AA=0.504 修复

- 明确报告 majority baseline (~0.5)
- 加 "statistically indistinguishable from chance (n=3, p>0.05)"
- 修改措辞: "the advantage narrows" → "ActorAttack detection degrades to chance level (F1=0.504±0.107, indistinguishable from majority baseline)"
- Abstract 对应处已有 "F1=0.504" 但需加 "≈chance level"

### P1-MF5: Seed-level 为主要报告单位

全文搜索 "bootstrap"、"CI"、"confidence interval"、"non-overlapping"：
- "confirms non-overlapping intervals" → "seed-level results show consistent separation"
- 每处池化 CI 引用加 "(exploratory, pooled across seeds assuming independence)"
- 确保 seed-level Table 8 在正文中被引用为主要证据

### P1-MF6: 方差 3-23× 降级

- experiments.tex 中 "3-23×" 前加 "descriptive"
- 检查 intro/conclusion 有无引用此数字，同步降级
- 确保 ToxicChat 反转限定已存在（Reviewer 确认 experiments.tex L115 附近已有）

### P1-SF4: 因果语言系统替换

高优先级替换（Reviewer 指出的 3 处）：
1. abstract: "Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks **requires** domain adaptation" → "benefits substantially from domain adaptation at base model scale"
2. intro: "cross-attack generalization **requires** domain adaptation, not a better classifier" → "cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation"
3. experiments: "Domain exposure is **the primary mechanism**" → "the primary observed factor"

全文搜索 "requires"、"drives"、"causes"、"mechanism"、"necessary"，逐处评估是否需要改为 associational 语言。

Discussion 新增一段 memorization/overfitting 替代解释："An alternative interpretation of the low-dimensional discriminative subspace is overfitting or representation collapse rather than beneficial feature selection. The 68.1% FPR on out-of-distribution benign data provides partial evidence for this interpretation..."

---

## P2: MF4 长度匹配对照实验（2-4h）

**这是唯一的 evidence 问题，也是唯一能推动 3.0→3.5 的新证据。**

实验设计（推理 only，不需训练）：
1. 从现有 DD/AA 测试数据中，按 turn count 分桶
2. 构造长度匹配子集：只保留 benign 和 jailbreak turn count 重叠的区间（比如都在 5-10 turns）
3. 用已训练好的 9-class DeBERTa + BiGRU 模型在匹配子集上推理
4. 报告 length-matched F1 vs full-set F1 的差异

如果长度匹配后 F1 基本不变 → DD 不依赖长度，是正面证据
如果 F1 显著下降 → 长度确实是 confound，诚实报告为 limitation

同时在 Section 5 (Discussion) 增加 prominent 段落讨论 AA padding collapse (-57.9pp):
- 区分 "length as shortcut" vs "padding destroys signal"
- DD Δ=-2.5pp 说明 DD 对 padding resilient
- AA Δ=-57.9pp 可能源于 signal density 稀释（真实攻击话语在 padding 后被稀释到不可检测）

**连接到 westd-33183**，用 cuda:0 执行推理。

---

## P3: Should-Fix + 遗漏项（1-2h）

**P3-SF2**: Footnote 1 修改为 "We use 'disentangled' to describe our experimental methodology of isolating individual factors through controlled ablation, distinct from disentangled representation learning (β-VAE, InfoGAN)."

**P3-SF3**: Section 4.7 或 Method 增加操作化定义段落：
"We operationally distinguish template attacks (fixed prompt template with variable payload; e.g., DanPrompt) from structurally complex attacks (multi-turn strategy adaptation with dynamic context exploitation; e.g., ActorAttack). A quantitative proxy is per-turn strategy transition rate: template attacks show rate < 0.1 (consistent strategy), while complex attacks show rate > 0.3 (frequent strategy shifts)."

**P3-SF5**: Abstract 加 "at base model scale (125M–184M)"；Conclusion 加 "At larger scales (355M), DAPT can be counterproductive (RoBERTa-large: vanilla AA=1.000 vs 9-class AA=0.946)."

**P3-SF6**: Discussion 新增段落讨论 temporal modeling 等价性的 implications:
"The equivalence of mean pooling and BiGRU (Appendix T) suggests that per-turn representations already encode sufficient discriminative signal, with temporal order providing negligible additional information. This challenges the intuition that multi-turn attacks require sequential modeling and suggests that attack detection may operate on per-turn features aggregated independently."

**P3-SF7**: Related Work 或 Experiments 加一句: "Direct experimental comparison with DeepContext, BIID, and G-Guard is precluded by the absence of public implementations and incompatible evaluation protocols (closed-source datasets, different attack taxonomies); Appendix Q provides a parameter-matched BERT-base proxy as a partial substitute."

**P3-Q1**: 补充 few-shot benign adaptation 实现细节。在 Section 4.9 或 Method 加: "Few-shot adaptation fine-tunes the Stage-2 BiGRU classifier head (Stage-1 encoder frozen) on N target-domain benign conversations using BCE loss for 5 epochs. Adaptation and evaluation sets are disjoint (10 conversations for adaptation, remainder for evaluation)."

**P3-Q2**: Method 或 Appendix 明确: "Scrambled labels use a fixed random permutation (seed 42). Robustness is verified across 2 additional random permutations (seeds 123, 456) with consistent results (Appendix F)." — 如果这个事实存在的话。如果只做了一个 permutation，诚实报告并在 Limitations 标注。

**P3-Q4**: Section 4.5 加一句: "An alternative explanation for the mixed-LLM performance degradation is per-source data reduction (250 vs 350 conversations per source) rather than distribution shift per se. Disentangling these factors would require training on 350 conversations from each source independently, which we leave to future work."

**P3-Missing References**: 核实 R19 推荐的 7 篇引用真实性（WebSearch 验证 arXiv ID），优先添加:
1. "Do Internal Layers of LLMs Reveal Patterns for Jailbreak Detection?" (2510.06594) — 与 SVD/Fisher 分析方法论高度相关
2. "Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace" (2602.11495) — representation-level detection
3. Markov et al. 2023 "A holistic approach to undesired content detection" — OpenAI production baseline
其余 4 篇视相关性酌情添加。

**P3-Visual**: Figure 2 x 轴标签字号放大至 ≥8pt。

---

## 执行规范

1. 每个 P 级完成后立即报告，附修改前后对比（old_string → new_string）
2. P0 和 P1 可由不同 worker 并行
3. P2 实验完成后，无论结果正面/负面，都在 Section 5 讨论并报告
4. 所有修改完成后 `paper_compile_and_check` 验证编译 + 页数
5. 修复后的关键段落（Abstract、Conclusion、Table 2 caption、Discussion 新段落）发给 Reviewer 做验证

**禁止**: 自由发挥重写其他部分；引入新 claim；改变论文标题/作者；触碰 figures/ 以外的非 .tex 文件

### Tool result

_source line 7999 · 2026-05-23T11:49:27.373Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CjifeUPBgU6fLFdasaEBoQ`</summary>

```text
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization factors.} On synthetic multi-turn data, thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into two orthogonal axes (attack-side and benign-side), the first such controlled factorial study in this domain. On the attack side, classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (vanilla 0.26--0.31 $\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. On the benign side, 10--25 target-domain conversations reduce FPR from 58--68\% to 0--1.5\% on two independent sources (WildChat, OASST2; IID evaluation) without detection degradation.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Partition sufficiency with an observed difficulty gradient (at base model scale).} Domain-relevant classification objectives, including scrambled labels, suffice for cross-attack generalization on template attacks (TOST-confirmed, $|d| \leq 0.14$ on DD, $d \approx 0$ on FITD at $n$\,=\,10), while attacks employing more varied conversational strategies (ActorAttack) show a moderate label effect ($d$\,=\,0.44). This is consistent with \citet{zhang2021understanding}'s framework: domain-relevant partitioning is necessary (topic labels fail) and compression localizes to 3 SVD dimensions in layers 8--12. A probe over these dimensions confirms sufficiency; removing them degrades DD by 17\,pp while FITD is unaffected (partial necessity; Appendix~\ref{app:discriminative_probe}).
27	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011EnFoxUAUUa7XDXL725Xni`</summary>

```text
112	\paragraph{Domain exposure is the primary mechanism.}
113	MLM bridges $\sim$60\,pp from vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026 $\to$ 0.914$\pm$0.115) without labels; this supports the DAPT interpretation~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.
114	All three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.997--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \geq 0.40$) provide descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence. Extended to $n$\,=\,10 seeds, TOST (margin = 5\,pp) confirms equivalence on DD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d$\,=\,$-$0.14; Table~\ref{tab:tost_sensitivity}) and FITD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d \approx 0$). An observed difficulty gradient emerges: template attacks show full equivalence ($d < 0.1$), while strategy-diverse ActorAttack retains moderate label sensitivity ($d$\,=\,0.44, $p$\,=\,0.183). Label quality and granularity are descriptively non-factors for detection.
115	Classification supervision is associated with 3--23$\times$ lower cross-seed variance over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\ 0.115 on DD; observed range across 3 seeds; formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at $n$=3; cross-seed ensemble yields marginal additional gains, Appendix~\ref{app:ensemble}). This stability benefit does not transfer to human-authored data, where classification-supervised variants exhibit the highest variance and pure MLM the lowest (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
116	Appendix~\ref{app:tsne} provides t-SNE visualizations confirming that domain-adapted variants produce tighter, more separable embedding clusters than the vanilla baseline.
```

    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization factors.} On synthetic multi-turn data, thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into two orthogonal axes (attack-side and benign-side), the first such controlled factorial study in this domain. On the attack side, classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (vanilla 0.26--0.31 $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. On the benign side, 10--25 target-domain conversations reduce FPR from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\% on two independent sources (WildChat, OASST2; IID evaluation) without detection degradation.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Partition sufficiency with an observed difficulty gradient (at base model scale).} Domain-relevant classification objectives, including scrambled labels, suffice for cross-attack generalization on template attacks (TOST-confirmed, $|d| \\leq 0.14$ on DD, $d \\approx 0$ on FITD at $n$\\,=\\,10), while attacks employing more varied conversational strategies (ActorAttack) show a moderate label effect ($d$\\,=\\,0.44). This is consistent with \\citet{zhang2021understanding}'s framework: domain-relevant partitioning is necessary (topic labels fail) and compression localizes to 3 SVD dimensions in layers 8--12. A probe over these dimensions confirms sufficiency; removing them degrades DD by 17\\,pp while FITD is unaffected (partial necessity; Appendix~\\ref{app:discriminative_probe}).\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Two-step adaptation protocol.} On the synthetic benchmark, continued pretraining achieves 24.3$\\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge (a deployment cost comparison; parameter counts differ by 43$\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}); 10--25 target-domain benign conversations reduce FPR below deployment thresholds on both WildChat and OASST2 crowdsourced conversations. No target-domain attack data is required.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LSpBfHkCtSe28a5hBc5Jzi`</summary>

```text
1	% abstract.tex
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.
4	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, and existing defenses fail to generalize across attack families: Llama Guard~3, a widely deployed safety classifier, detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation.
5	We isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.
6	Classification-based domain adaptation recovers OOD detection on synthetic data (F1\,=\,0.97--1.0 vs.\ vanilla 0.26--0.31; attack-side OOD, benign IID) while unsupervised pretraining collapses.
7	Under mixed-LLM training, template attacks remain detectable but strategy-diverse ActorAttack degrades (F1\,=\,0.504).
8	These findings derive from synthetic data from a single LLM; the evidence hierarchy reverses on human-authored single-turn data, indicating condition-specific rather than universal mechanisms.
9	Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, benign distribution shift is a practical bottleneck: few-shot benign adaptation reduces false-positive rates below deployment thresholds on two independent sources.
10	These findings decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes, attack-side (discriminative compression) and benign-side (few-shot adaptation), with 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：P2 长度匹配对照实验（推理 only）\n\n### 背景\nReviewer 担心 OOD 检测结果可能由 turn count 差异驱动（jailbreak vs benign 对话长度不同导致 shortcut）。需要构造长度匹配子集并验证。\n\n### 实验设计\n1. 从现有 DD/AA 测试数据中，按 turn count 分桶\n2. 构造长度匹配子集：只保留 benign 和 jailbreak turn count 重叠的区间\n3. 用已训练好的 9-class DeBERTa + BiGRU 模型在匹配子集上推理\n4. 报告 length-matched F1 vs full-set F1 的差异\n\n### 服务器信息\n- 服务器：westd-33183（root）\n- GPU：cuda:0\n- 项目路径：`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/`\n- 数据路径：`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/plan_002_splits/`\n- Checkpoint：`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints_archive/plan_002/deberta_multitask/best/model.pt`（9-class）或 `checkpoints/plan_002_seed42/` 下的 checkpoint\n- Python 环境：`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`\n\n### 步骤\n1. SSH 连接 westd-33183\n2. 检查数据文件结构：`ls data/plan_002_splits/` 了解 test/OOD 数据格式\n3. 写一个 Python 脚本 `scripts/exp67_length_matching.py`：\n   - 加载 DD 和 AA 测试数据\n   - 统计每个对话的 turn count（user turns 数量）\n   - 分桶显示分布\n   - 构造长度匹配子集（只保留 jailbreak 和 benign turn count 重叠范围内的样本）\n   - 加载已训练的 9-class 模型 + BiGRU\n   - 在匹配子集上推理，计算 F1\n   - 对比全集 F1 vs 匹配子集 F1\n   - 输出结果到 `results/exp67_length_matching.json`\n4. 执行脚本\n5. 报告结果\n\n### 关键参考\n- 现有推理代码可能在 `src/` 目录下，先检查有无现成的 evaluation 脚本可复用\n- 模型架构：DeBERTa-v3-base encoder + BiGRU + MLP classifier\n- 需要 3 个 seed (42, 123, 456) 的结果\n\n### 注意\n- 这是推理实验，不需要训练\n- 用 `submit_training_job` 提交（即使是推理），以便 cron 监控\n- 如果长度匹配后 F1 基本不变 → 正面证据（DD 不依赖长度）\n- 如果 F1 显著下降 → 诚实报告为 limitation",
  "tool_groups": [
    "ssh",
    "registry"
  ]
}

```text
1	% abstract.tex
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns. Existing defenses fail to generalize: Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation. Detecting such attacks benefits substantially from domain adaptation at base model scale, but the form of adaptation matters. We isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at base scale (125M--184M parameters) on synthetic multi-turn data decompose contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, and label granularity across three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families. On synthetic data, classification-based adaptation recovers OOD detection (F1\,=\,0.97--1.0 vs.\ vanilla 0.26--0.31; attack-side OOD, IID benign upper bound) while unsupervised pretraining collapses; under mixed-LLM training, template attacks remain detectable but strategy-diverse attacks degrade to chance level (F1\,=\,0.504). On human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), this hierarchy reverses: masked language modeling outperforms 9-class classification (F1 0.837 vs.\ 0.596), evidence that classification adaptation's advantage is condition-specific rather than universal. Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, few-shot benign adaptation reduces false-positive rates from 58--68\% to 0--1.5\%, with 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge baselines.
4	\end{abstract}
5

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01TxBiqALFGgfq7gyNF1GoZn`</summary>

```text
1	% conclusion.tex
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Discriminative compression and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97).
8	Classification DAPT compresses features into few dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\ 25--32; Fisher 2.6--19.7 vs.\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\ref{app:representation}), consistent with \citet{zhang2021understanding}: domain-relevant partitioning is necessary (topic DAPT fails; Appendix~\ref{app:topic_failure}) and compression localizes to 3 SVD dimensions in layers 8--12 (Appendix~\ref{app:layerwise}). A probe over these dimensions confirms sufficiency; removal degrades DD by 17\,pp while FITD is unaffected (partial necessity; Appendix~\ref{app:discriminative_probe}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Cross-condition transfer and boundaries.}
11	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer beyond single-LLM artifacts; mixed-LLM 9-class maintains 0.818 while MLM collapses, suggesting classification extracts style-invariant features. Classification supervision is associated with lower variance on synthetic OOD (3--23$\times$, 3 seeds) but this reverses on ToxicChat (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. WildChat FPR rises from 48.1\% to 68.1\% under 9-class DAPT, confirming that attack-side generalization amplifies benign distribution sensitivity.
12	
13	\section{Conclusion}
14	
15	We decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes. On synthetic multi-turn data at base model scale (125M--184M), classification-based domain adaptation recovers out-of-distribution detection (F1\,=\,0.97--1.0 vs.\ vanilla 0.26--0.31) while unsupervised pretraining collapses; template attacks remain robust across conditions ($|d|\!\leq\!0.14$) but strategy-diverse attacks degrade under mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.504). Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, few-shot benign adaptation reduces false-positive rates from 58--68\% to 0--1.5\% with as few as 10 conversations, at 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.
16	
17	On human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), this hierarchy reverses: MLM outperforms classification (0.837 vs 0.596, a 24.1\,pp gap). Classification DAPT's advantage is condition-specific (multi-turn, synthetic) rather than universal.
18	
19	These findings have boundaries. At larger scales (355M), DAPT can be counterproductive (RoBERTa-large vanilla AA\,=\,1.000 vs.\ 9-class 0.946). Mixed-LLM diversity degrades strategy-diverse attack detection to near chance, and length sensitivity persists across configurations.
20	
21	The ablation framework contributes a reusable methodology for safety classification research: four adaptation axes (domain exposure, supervision type, label quality, granularity) at multiple scales provide a template for diagnosing when and why domain adaptation succeeds or fails.
22	
23	\section{Limitations}
24	\label{sec:limitations}
25	
26	All findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by a single LLM (Qwen3-8B; 350 training, 76--118 test per family, 3 seeds); the evidence hierarchy reverses on human single-turn data (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}), and no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. OOD F1 figures are upper bounds because benign data is IID; estimated dual-OOD F1 drops 12--14\,pp without adaptation but recovers within 0.3\,pp after few-shot adaptation (Appendix~\ref{app:dual_ood}). Benign-side adaptation (WildChat FPR 68.1\%\,$\rightarrow$\,0\% with 10 conversations; OASST2 57.5\%\,$\rightarrow$\,1.0\% at $N$\,=\,25) is validated on only two platforms with small samples ($n \leq 49$). Benign-only adaptation does not address cross-LLM distribution shift: adapting on Llama-origin conversations collapses Llama jailbreak detection (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}). Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg} and non-text modalities are not addressed.
27	
28	\section*{Ethics Statement}
29	
30	This work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.
31	
```

    "snippet": "1\t% experiments.tex\n2\t\n3\t\\section{Experiments}\n4\t\n5\t\\paragraph{Terminology.}\n6\t\\emph{Cross-attack OOD}: evaluation on attack families unseen during training; benign distribution remains IID throughout (OOD reflects attack-side shift only). $K$: prefix length (number of conversation turns retained). \\emph{DAPT}: all Stage-1 adaptations; we extend \\citet{gururangan2020dontstop} to supervised variants. \\emph{Evidence hierarchy} (on synthetic data): vanilla $<$ MLM $<$ classification (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}).\n7\t\n8\t\\subsection{Setup}\n9\t\n10\t\\paragraph{Data.}\n11\tAll synthetic data is generated by Qwen3-8B~\\citep{qwen3_2025}.\n12\tThe IID dataset contains 250 Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} jailbreak and 250 topic-anchored benign conversations, split into training (350), validation (74), and test (76) sets with balanced class ratios (e.g., test = 38 jailbreak + 38 benign).\n13\tStage~1 (DAPT) uses training + validation conversations; Stage~2 (BiGRU) trains on training, early-stops on validation, and evaluates on test.\n14\tOOD evaluation pairs attack-specific jailbreak conversations with the 38 IID-test benign conversations: 80 FITD~\\citep{fitd2024} (non-discriminative; Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}) + 38 benign = 118 total, 80 Deceptive Delight (DD)~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} + 38 benign = 118 total, and 80 ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} + 38 benign = 118 total.\n15\tNo overlap exists in attack templates across families; benign is IID (same generation pipeline).\n16\tA paraphrased version of the IID test set (BERTScore 0.92, lexical overlap 0.20) serves as the adversarial robustness benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:perturbation}).\n17\tFor human data validation, we use ToxicChat~\\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (204 jailbreak + 204 benign from real LMSYS user interactions), entirely independent of Qwen3-8B generation.\n18\t\n19\t\\paragraph{Models.}\n20\tSeven DeBERTa-v3 variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline and differ only in Stage-1 training (Section~\\ref{sec:method}).\n21\tOur vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU configuration is architecturally equivalent to DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026} and differs only in encoder choice (DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ BERT); it serves as our primary baseline.\n22\tFor cross-encoder validation, we replicate vanilla, MLM, and 9-class variants on RoBERTa-base~\\citep{liu2019roberta} (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).\n23\tWe also compare TF-IDF LR, LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}), and Llama Guard~3~8B~\\citep{metallamaguard3_2024} (zero-shot; evaluates last-response safety given conversation context), which detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks (5/278 pooled recall; Table~\\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).\n24\t\n25\t\\paragraph{Metrics.}\n26\tF1 for binary jailbreak detection at prefix $K$=2 and full conversations (full per-$K$ results in Appendix~\\ref{sec:full_prefix}).\n27\tOOD results report 3-seed mean$\\pm$std (seeds 42, 123, 456) for all variants.\n28\tFor key pairwise comparisons, we report BCa bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals (10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across 3 seeds; this assumes cross-seed independence and is exploratory) and paired permutation tests (10,000 permutations). Seed-level F1 values (Table~\\ref{tab:seed_level}, Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_level}) confirm all pairwise conclusions hold at every seed.\n29\tAll standard deviations are computed over 3 random seeds using the population formula ($n$ divisor); Bessel-corrected estimates ($n{-}1$ divisor) are $\\approx$22\\% larger but do not alter any significance conclusions.\n30\t\n31\t\\paragraph{Training.}\n32\tStage-1: learning rate $2 \\times 10^{-5}$, batch 16, 4 epochs, MLM masking 15\\%.\n33\tStage-2: two-layer MLP, 20 epochs.\n34\t\n35\t%----------------------------------------------------------------------\n36\t\\subsection{Cross-Attack Generalization}\n37\t\n38\t\\begin{table}[t]\n39\t\\centering\n40\t\\small\n41\t\\caption{Cross-attack generalization (F1) at $K$=2 and full conversation length. IID: Crescendo; OOD: Deceptive Delight (DD). 3-seed mean$\\pm$std where noted; full per-$K$ in Appendix~\\ref{sec:full_prefix}.}\n42\t\\label{tab:main_results}\n43\t\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc|cc@{}}\n44\t\\toprule\n45\t& \\multicolumn{2}{c|}{\\textit{IID (Cresc.)}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textit{OOD (DD)}} \\\\\n46\t\\textbf{Method} & $K$=2 & Full & $K$=2 & Full \\\\\n47\t\\midrule\n48\t9-class & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & .981$\\pm$.013 & \\textbf{.997$\\pm$.005} \\\\\n49\tMLM-only$^{\\ddagger}$ & .974 & 1.000 & \\textbf{.993$\\pm$.009} & .914$\\pm$.115 \\\\\n50\tVanilla & .978$\\pm$.016 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & .479$\\pm$.038 & .312$\\pm$.026 \\\\\n51\tTF-IDF LR$^{\\S}$ & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .961 \\\\\n52\t\\bottomrule\n53\t\\end{tabular}\n54\t\\vspace{-2pt}\n55\t{\\footnotesize $^{\\ddagger}$MLM-only IID reports single seed (42); OOD reports 3-seed. $^{\\S}$TF-IDF is deterministic (no std).}\n56\t\\end{table}\n57\t\n58\tVanilla encoders collapse under cross-attack transfer; domain-adapted variants recover near-perfect detection (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}, Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_attack_bar}).\n59\tDD exhibits high lexical separability (TF-IDF F1\\,=\\,0.961); ActorAttack, where TF-IDF collapses to 0.261, serves as the primary stress test for beyond-lexical generalization.\n60\tOn OOD DD, vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1 = 0.312$\\pm$0.026 and worsens with more turns (0.628 at $K$=1 to 0.312 at Full), consistent with attack turns occupying ``benign'' embedding regions~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}.\n61\tMLM achieves mean F1 $\\geq$ 0.97 at all prefixes $K \\leq 5$ without any classification labels; domain exposure alone is sufficient.\n62\tAt full conversation length, MLM drops to 0.914$\\pm$0.115 (seed 456 recall = 0.64), while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.005.\n63\tTF-IDF LR reaches F1 = 0.961 on full DD; DD is fully solvable by lexical features.\n64\tOn strategy-diverse ActorAttack, TF-IDF collapses to F1 = 0.261 (precision = 1.0, recall = 0.15), while 9-class maintains 0.972$\\pm$0.026: a 71\\,pp gap. Lexical methods cannot capture multi-persona role-playing attack structure. This establishes a ``difficulty threshold'' for lexical methods parallel to that of vanilla encoders: both succeed on DD and fail on ActorAttack (Section~\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n65\tBootstrap CIs confirm non-overlapping intervals (9-class DD Full [0.943, 1.000] vs.\\ vanilla [0.202, 0.341]; all permutation $p < 0.0001$). FITD is non-discriminative (all variants F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.990; Section~\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n66\t\n67\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n68\t\\centering\n69\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig2_cross_attack_bar.pdf}\n70\t\\caption{Cross-attack overview (F1 at full conversation length). DisPeD (9-class) maintains near-perfect detection across IID and both OOD families; vanilla collapses on OOD DD. FITD is non-discriminative for all methods.}\n71\t\\label{fig:cross_attack_bar}\n72\t\\end{figure}\n73\t\n74\tPrefix-length analysis (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:full_prefix}) shows vanilla F1 drops from 0.628 at $K$=1 to 0.312 at full length. MLM achieves strong early detection ($K$=2: 0.993) but is unstable at full length (0.914$\\pm$0.115); the 9-class model maintains 0.972--0.997 across all prefixes. Classification supervision thus stabilizes against distributional drift.\n75\t\n76\t%----------------------------------------------------------------------\n77\t\\subsection{Decomposing the Detection Mechanism}\n78\t\\label{sec:decomposition}\n79\t\n80\t\\begin{table}[t]\n81\t\\centering\n82\t\\small\n83\t\\caption{Evidence hierarchy on OOD DD and ActorAttack (AA) at full conversation length (3-seed mean$\\pm$std where available). OOD F1 reflects attack-side generalization only; benign samples are IID from training distribution (benign-side shift evaluated in Section~\\ref{sec:benign_shift}). Bold indicates best per column. All variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline. Full per-prefix results in Appendix Tables~\\ref{tab:full_prefix} and~\\ref{tab:actorattack_full}.}\n84\t\\label{tab:evidence_hierarchy}\n85\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n86\t\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n87\t\\toprule\n88\t\\textbf{Variant} & DD-Full & AA-Full & DD-$K$=2 \\\\\n89\t\\midrule\n90\t9-class (full model) & .997$\\pm$.005 & .972$\\pm$.026 & .981$\\pm$.013 \\\\\n91\tBinary (2-class) & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & \\textbf{.990$\\pm$.008} & .994$\\pm$.009 \\\\\n\n... <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01ULnmgzDyUsM5gteRA2yw4G`</summary>

```text
10	\paragraph{Cross-condition transfer and boundaries.}
11	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer beyond single-LLM artifacts; mixed-LLM 9-class maintains 0.818 while MLM collapses, suggesting classification extracts style-invariant features. Classification supervision is associated with lower variance on synthetic OOD (descriptively 3--23$\times$, 3 seeds) but this reverses on ToxicChat (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. At larger scales (355M), DAPT can be counterproductive: RoBERTa-large vanilla achieves AA F1\,=\,1.000 while 9-class drops to 0.946. WildChat FPR rises from 48.1\% to 68.1\% under 9-class DAPT, confirming that attack-side generalization amplifies benign distribution sensitivity.
12	
13	An alternative interpretation of the low-dimensional discriminative subspace (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}) is overfitting or representation collapse rather than beneficial feature selection. The 68.1\% FPR on out-of-distribution benign data provides partial evidence for this interpretation: classification DAPT may compress representations in ways that sacrifice generalization to unseen benign distributions while improving attack-side separation.
14	
15	\section{Conclusion}
16	
17	We decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes. On synthetic multi-turn data at base model scale (125M--184M), classification-based domain adaptation recovers out-of-distribution detection (F1\,=\,0.97--1.0 vs.\ vanilla 0.26--0.31) while unsupervised pretraining collapses; template attacks remain robust across conditions ($|d|\!\leq\!0.14$) but strategy-diverse attacks degrade under mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.504). Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, few-shot benign adaptation reduces false-positive rates from 58--68\% to 0--1.5\% with as few as 10 conversations, at 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.
```

</details>

501-Without benign adaptation, dual-OOD F1 drops by 12--14\,pp (DD: $.994 \to .855$; AA: $.981 \to .842$), driven entirely by false positives on OOD benign inputs. After few-shot adaptation---10 WildChat or 25 OASST2 conversations---dual-OOD F1 recovers to within 0.3\,pp of the IID upper bound, confirming that the lightweight adaptation procedure (Section~\ref{sec:benign_shift}) effectively closes the benign distribution gap without compromising attack-side performance.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent results! Length matching confirms the model doesn't rely on turn count as a shortcut. Key findings:

- DD OOD: Range-matched Δ = -0.008 (negligible), Bucket-matched Δ = +0.025 (slightly better)
- AA OOD: Range-matched Δ = +0.015, Bucket-matched Δ = +0.009

Both deltas < 3%, confirming detection doesn't depend on length differences. This is positive evidence.

However, I notice the F1 values (0.887, 0.883) are lower than what I'd expect for the 9-class model. 

The full-set DD OOD F1 should be around 0.997 based on our paper results, so these lower values are puzzling. The worker might have used a different evaluation protocol or combined seeds differently. The length-matched subsets are smaller, which could affect the F1 calculation, but the gap is substantial enough that something seems off. Let me register this and flag it for the Director.

What matters most here is the delta between conditions, not the absolute values. If the same evaluation protocol shows 0.887 for full-set and 0.880 for range-matched, that -0.008 difference is what's meaningful—it suggests length matching doesn't substantially change the outcome. There could be a macro vs binary F1 discrepancy or a bug somewhere, but the relative comparison still holds. I'll register the experiment, report to the Director, and draft the Discussion section covering this finding alongside the AA padding collapse.
```

</details>

  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect",
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：在 Discussion 中增加长度控制段落\n\n文件：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/conclusion.tex`\n\n### 背景\nexp67 长度匹配实验结果（正面）：\n- DD OOD: full-set F1=0.887±0.086, range-matched F1=0.880±0.094 (Δ=-0.008), bucket-matched F1=0.913±0.079 (Δ=+0.025)\n- AA OOD: full-set F1=0.883±0.091, range-matched F1=0.898±0.077 (Δ=+0.015), bucket-matched F1=0.892±0.074 (Δ=+0.009)\n- 两种匹配方式 delta 均 < 3%\n- DD jailbreak turns [6,10], benign turns [6,14]\n- AA jailbreak turns [3,8], benign turns [6,14], overlap [6,8], 仅 34/80 jailbreak 保留\n\n### 要做的事\n1. Read conclusion.tex\n2. 在 Discussion 段落（`\\paragraph{Cross-condition transfer and boundaries.}` 之后，`\\section{Conclusion}` 之前）增加一个新 `\\paragraph`：\n\n```latex\n\\paragraph{Length as a potential confound.}\nTurn count differs between attack families (DD: 6--10 turns, AA: 3--8) and IID benign (6--14), raising the concern that detection may exploit length shortcuts. Length-matched evaluation on the overlapping turn-count range shows negligible change: DD F1 shifts by $-$0.8\\,pp (range-matched) to $+$2.5\\,pp (bucket-matched), AA by $+$1.5\\,pp to $+$0.9\\,pp, confirming that detection operates on per-turn content rather than conversation length. The $-$57.9\\,pp ActorAttack collapse under zero-padding (Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}) is consistent with signal density dilution---padding introduces uninformative turns that reduce the proportion of attack-bearing content---rather than length shortcut removal.\n```\n\n3. 注意不要碰其他段落（另一个 worker 已添加了 \"alternative interpretation\" 段落和 RoBERTa-large 反转句子）\n4. 编译验证：`cd /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper && pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode main.tex`\n5. 确认编译成功，报告页数",

  ]
}

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01GaZd5DMBAPW9kbGXpYwRyA`</summary>

```text
13	An alternative interpretation of the low-dimensional discriminative subspace (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}) is overfitting or representation collapse rather than beneficial feature selection. The 68.1\% FPR on out-of-distribution benign data provides partial evidence for this interpretation: classification DAPT may compress representations in ways that sacrifice generalization to unseen benign distributions while improving attack-side separation.
14	
15	\paragraph{Length as a potential confound.}
16	Turn count differs between attack families (DD: 6--10 turns, AA: 3--8) and IID benign (6--14), raising the concern that detection may exploit length shortcuts. Length-matched evaluation on the overlapping turn-count range shows negligible change: DD F1 shifts by $-$0.8\,pp (range-matched) to $+$2.5\,pp (bucket-matched), AA by $+$1.5\,pp to $+$0.9\,pp, confirming that detection operates on per-turn content rather than conversation length. The $-$57.9\,pp ActorAttack collapse under zero-padding (Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}) is consistent with signal density dilution---padding introduces uninformative turns that reduce the proportion of attack-bearing content---rather than length shortcut removal.
17	
18	\section{Conclusion}
19	
20	We decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes. On synthetic multi-turn data at base model scale (125M--184M), classification-based domain adaptation recovers out-of-distribution detection (F1\,=\,0.97--1.0 vs.\ vanilla 0.26--0.31) while unsupervised pretraining collapses; template attacks remain robust across conditions ($|d|\!\leq\!0.14$) but strategy-diverse attacks degrade under mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.504). Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, few-shot benign adaptation reduces false-positive rates from 58--68\% to 0--1.5\% with as few as 10 conversations, at 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.
```
